T h3 Inclusive Sans 26px/500 #344948 @120,962 844x18 :: 1. Wholly Owned Subsidiary
T p Inter 16px/300 #344948 @120,1000 844x58 :: A foreign company can establish a wholly owned subsidiary in India by incorporating a private limited company under the Companies Act, 2013. This structure may be suitable where 100% Foreign Direct Investment (FDI) is permitted in the relevant sector, subject to applicable conditions.
T h3 Inclusive Sans 26px/500 #344948 @120,1098 844x18 :: 2. Joint Venture (JV)
T p Inter 16px/300 #344948 @120,1136 844x58 :: A foreign company can enter into a joint venture with an Indian company or investor. This structure allows the parties to share ownership, resources, expertise, and business responsibilities, subject to applicable FDI and regulatory requirements.
T h3 Inclusive Sans 26px/500 #344948 @120,1233 844x18 :: 3. Liaison Office
T p Inter 16px/300 #344948 @120,1272 844x58 :: A liaison office can be established to represent the foreign parent company, explore business opportunities, facilitate communication, and promote its interests in India. A liaison office generally cannot undertake commercial activities or earn income in India.
T h3 Inclusive Sans 26px/500 #344948 @120,1369 844x18 :: 4. Branch Office
T p Inter 16px/300 #344948 @120,1407 844x58 :: A branch office is an extension of the foreign company and may undertake permitted activities such as export and import, consultancy, research, and other activities approved under applicable regulations. Its activities are subject to the conditions prescribed by the relevant authorities.
T h3 Inclusive Sans 26px/500 #344948 @120,1505 844x18 :: 5. Project Office
T p Inter 16px/300 #344948 @120,1543 844x58 :: A foreign company may establish a project office in India for executing a specific project or contract. This structure can be suitable for foreign businesses involved in construction, infrastructure, engineering, and other project-based activities.

T h3 Inclusive Sans 26px/500 #344948 @120,800 844x18 :: What is a Sole Proprietorship?
T p Inter 16px/300 #344948 @120,838 844x77 :: A sole proprietorship is a business owned and managed by one individual. The proprietor is personally responsible for the business’s debts and liabilities. Unlike a company or LLP, it does not have a separate legal identity from its owner. In India, there is no single central registration for a sole proprietorship; applicable registrations depend on the nature and location of the business.
